is a real and reversibility preserving Toeplitz in time operator. Moreover, we obtain from (
4.21
) in Lemma
4.8
, (
6.22
), (
5.25
), (
6.11
) and the smallness condition (
6.19
),

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}\mathscr{B}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,1}\|_{q,H_{\varphi,\theta,\eta}^{s+s_{0}+2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\beta\|_{q,s+s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,1}\|_{q,H_{\varphi,\theta,\eta}^{s_{0}}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+s_{0}+2+\sigma_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.47)

Applying Lemma
4.1
, we get from (
5.2
), Lemma
4.2
and (
6.12
),

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathbb{K}_{\varepsilon r,1}\|_{q,H_{\varphi,\theta,\eta}^{s}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{12}v_{\varepsilon r,1}\|_{q,H_{\varphi,\theta,\eta}^{s}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\Big(\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{j\in\{1,2\}}\|\mathfrak{I}_{j}\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\Big).

Added to Lemma
4.8
-(ii), (
6.28
), (
6.22
), (
5.25
) and (
6.19
), we infer

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\|\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}\mathscr{B}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}+1+\sigma_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.48)

The next task is to estimate the term
\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{\varepsilon r,1}\mathscr{B}
in (
6.34
). Note that (
5.24
), (
6.23
) and Lemma
4.8
imply that
\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{\varepsilon r,1}\mathscr{B}
is a real and reversibility preserving Toeplitz in time operator. In addition, Lemma
4.8
together with the estimates (
5.26
), (
6.11
) and (
6.22
) give

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{\varepsilon r,1}\mathscr{B}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathscr{K}_{\varepsilon r,1}\|_{q,H_{\varphi,\theta,\eta}^{s+2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\beta\|_{q,s+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\mathscr{K}_{\varepsilon r,1}\|_{q,H_{\varphi,\theta,\eta}^{s_{0}}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+s_{0}+2+\sigma_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.49)

Applying Lemma
4.1
, we get from (
5.2
) and (
6.12
),

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{12}\mathscr{K}_{\varepsilon r,1}\|_{q,H_{\varphi,\theta,\eta}^{s}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\Big(\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\max_{i\in\{1,2\}}\|\mathfrak{I}_{i}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\Big).

Then, combining Lemma
4.8
-(ii), (
6.28
), (
6.22
), (
5.26
) and (
6.19
), we get

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\|\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathcal{B}^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{\varepsilon r,1}\mathscr{B}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}+1+\sigma_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.50)

In view of (
6.34
), Lemma
4.8
and the previous computations, we conclude that
\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}
is a real and reversibility preserving toeplitz in time integral operator which satisfies, by (
6.40
), (
6.45
), (
6.2.1
) and (
6.2.1
),

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+s_{0}+3+\sigma_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

In addition, combining (
6.41
), (
6.46
), (
6.48
) and (
6.50
) yields

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\|\Delta_{12}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+s_{0}+2+\sigma_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

This ends the proof of Proposition
6.2
.
∎

6.2.2
Projection in the normal directions

In this section, we study the effects of the localization in the normal directions for the reduction of the transport part. For that purpose, we consider the localized quasi-periodic symplectic change of coordinates defined by

\mathscr{B}_{\perp}=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}.

Then, the main result of this section reads as follows.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
(\gamma,q,d,\tau_{1},s_{0},s_{h},\overline{s}_{h},\mathtt{p},S)
satisfy the assumptions (
1.11
), (
1.10
) and (
6.18
).

There exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and
\sigma_{3}=\sigma_{3}(\tau_{1},q,d,s_{0})\geqslant\sigma_{2}
such that if

\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\leqslant\varepsilon\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{h}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant 1,

(6.51)

then the following assertions hold true.

(i)

The operators
\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{\pm 1}
satisfy the following estimate

\|\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{\pm 1}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.52)

(ii)

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*},
in the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
introduced in Proposition
6.2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}

\displaystyle=\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+V_{i_{0}}^{\infty}\partial_{\theta}+\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{b}\ast\cdot\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{R}_{0}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}

\displaystyle:=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{D}_{0}+\mathscr{R}_{0}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}

\displaystyle:=\mathscr{L}_{0}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1},

where
\mathscr{R}_{0}=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{R}_{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}
is reversible and
\mathscr{D}_{0}=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{D}_{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}
is a reversible Fourier multiplier operator given by

\forall(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c},\hskip 10.00002pt\mathscr{D}_{0}\mathbf{e}_{l,j}={\rm i}\,\mu_{j}^{0}\,\mathbf{e}_{l,j},

with

\mu_{j}^{0}(b,\omega,i_{0})=\Omega_{j}(b)+jr^{1}(b,\omega,i_{0})\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{ and }\hskip 10.00002ptr^{1}(b,\omega,i_{0})=V_{i_{0}}^{\infty}(b,\omega)-\tfrac{1}{2}

(6.53)

and such that

\|r^{1}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\|\Delta_{12}r^{1}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.54)

(iii)

The operator
\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}
satisfies the following estimate

\|\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.55)

(iv)

The operator
\mathscr{R}_{0}
is a real and reversible Toeplitz in time operator satisfying

\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\hskip 10.00002pt\max_{k\in\{0,1\}}\|\partial_{\theta}^{k}\mathscr{R}_{0}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

(6.56)

and

\|\Delta_{12}\mathscr{R}_{0}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\mathtt{p}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.57)

(v)

Furthermore the operator
\mathscr{L}_{0}
satisfies

\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\hskip 10.00002pt\|\mathscr{L}_{0}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\rho\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.58)

Proof.

(i)
Follows from (
6.21
) and Lemma
4.1
-(ii).

(ii)
From (
6.7
) and the decomposition
\textnormal{Id}=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}+\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}
we write

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}-\varepsilon\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R})\mathscr{B}_{\perp}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}-\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}-\varepsilon\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}.

According to the definitions of
\mathfrak{L}_{\varepsilon r}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}
seen in Proposition
6.2
and in Lemma
3.1
and using (
5.21
), (
5.22
) and (
3.10
), one has in the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})

\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{B}\mathfrak{L}_{\varepsilon r}\hskip 10.00002pt\hbox{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon r}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{\varepsilon r}\cdot\right)+\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{b}\ast\cdot+\partial_{\theta}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}-\partial_{\theta}\mathbf{S}_{\varepsilon r,1}

and therefore

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}=

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\mathfrak{L}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}-\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\left(\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{\varepsilon r}\cdot\right)+\partial_{\theta}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}-\partial_{\theta}\mathbf{S}_{\varepsilon r,1}\right)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}-\varepsilon\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}_{\perp},

where we have used the identities

\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}=\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\hskip 10.00002pt\hbox{and}\hskip 10.00002pt[\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp},T]=0=[\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}},T],

for any Fourier multiplier
T
. The structure of
\mathfrak{L}_{\varepsilon r}
is detailed in Proposition
6.2
, and from this we deduce that

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\mathfrak{L}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+V_{i_{0}}^{\infty}\partial_{\theta}+\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{b}\ast\cdot+\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+V_{i_{0}}^{\infty}\partial_{\theta}+\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{b}\ast\cdot\big)+\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+V_{i_{0}}^{\infty}\partial_{\theta}+\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{b}\ast\cdot\big)+\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\mathfrak{L}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+V_{i_{0}}^{\infty}\partial_{\theta}+\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{b}\ast\cdot\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+V_{i_{0}}^{\infty}\partial_{\theta}+\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{b}\ast\cdot\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}.

Consequently, in the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
, one has the following reduction

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}=

\displaystyle\big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+V_{i_{0}}^{\infty}\partial_{\theta}+\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{b}\ast\cdot\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{R}_{\varepsilon r}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle-\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\left(\partial_{\theta}\left(V_{\varepsilon r}\cdot\right)+\partial_{\theta}\mathbf{L}_{\varepsilon r,1}-\partial_{\theta}\mathbf{S}_{\varepsilon r,1}\right)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}\mathscr{B}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}-\varepsilon\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{R}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}+\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{D}_{0}+\mathscr{R}_{0}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1},

(6.59)

where we set

\mathscr{D}_{0}:=\big(V_{i_{0}}^{\infty}\partial_{\theta}+\partial_{\theta}\mathcal{K}_{b}\ast\cdot\big)\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

and

\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}:=\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{B}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}.

(6.60)

(iii)
Results from (
6.60
), (
6.52
), (
6.21
), (
6.25
) and Lemma
4.1
-(ii).

(iv)

For the estimates (
6.56
) and (
6.57
), we refer to Lemma
[
47
, Prop 6.3 and Lem. 6.3]
. They are based on suitable duality representations of
\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{\pm 1}
linked to
\mathscr{B}^{\pm 1}.

(v)
It is obtained by (
5.27
), (
6.20
), (
6.56
) and Lemma
4.5
-(iv).
∎

6.2.3
Elimination of the remainder term

We perform here the KAM reduction of the remainder
\mathscr{R}_{0}
of Proposition
6.3
. This procedure allows to diagonalise the linearized operator in the normal directions, namely to conjugate it to a constant coefficients operator
\mathscr{L}_{\infty},
up to fast decaying terms.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
(\gamma,q,d,\tau_{1},\tau_{2},s_{0},s_{l},\overline{s}_{l},\overline{s}_{h},\overline{\mu}_{2},S)
satisfy (
1.11
), (
1.10
), (
6.16
). For any
(\mu_{2},s_{h})
satisfying

\displaystyle\mu_{2}\geqslant\overline{\mu}_{2}+2\tau_{2}q+2\tau_{2},\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002pts_{h}\geqslant\frac{3}{2}\mu_{2}+\overline{s}_{l}+1,

(6.61)

there exist
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
and
\sigma_{4}=\sigma_{4}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},q,d)\geqslant\sigma_{3}
such that if

\varepsilon\gamma^{-2-q}N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{h}+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant 1,

(6.62)

then the following assertions hold true.

(i)

There exists a family of invertible linear operator
\Phi_{\infty}:\mathcal{O}\to\mathcal{L}\big(H^{s}\cap L^{2}_{\perp}\big)
satisfying the estimates

\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{ }\|\Phi_{\infty}^{\pm 1}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.63)

There exists a diagonal operator
\mathscr{L}_{\infty}=\mathscr{L}_{\infty}(b,\omega,i_{0})
taking the form

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{D}_{\infty}

where
\mathscr{D}_{\infty}=\mathscr{D}_{\infty}(b,\omega,i_{0})=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{D}_{\infty}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}
is a reversible Fourier multiplier operator given by,

\forall(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c},\hskip 10.00002pt\mathscr{D}_{\infty}\mathbf{e}_{l,j}={\rm i}\,\mu_{j}^{\infty}\,\mathbf{e}_{l,j},

with

\forall j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c},\hskip 10.00002pt\mu_{j}^{\infty}(b,\omega,i_{0})=\mu_{j}^{0}(b,\omega,i_{0})+r_{j}^{\infty}(b,\omega,i_{0})

(6.64)

and

\displaystyle\sup_{j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}|j|\|r_{j}^{\infty}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}

(6.65)

such that in the Cantor set

\displaystyle\mathscr{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}(i_{0}):=\bigcap_{\underset{|l|\leqslant N_{n}}{(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}}\atop(l,j)\neq(0,j_{0})}\Big\{

\displaystyle(b,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0}),\big|\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty}(b,\omega,i_{0})-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty}(b,\omega,i_{0})\big|>\tfrac{2\gamma\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}\Big\}

we have

\displaystyle\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\mathscr{L}_{0}\Phi_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{L}_{\infty}+\mathtt{E}^{2}_{n},

and the linear operator
\mathtt{E}_{n}^{2}
satisfies the estimate

\|\mathtt{E}^{2}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}N_{0}^{{\mu}_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.66)

Notice that the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
was introduced in Proposition
6.2
, the operator
\mathscr{L}_{0}
and the frequencies
\big(\mu_{j}^{0}(b,\omega,i_{0})\big)_{j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}
were stated in Proposition
6.3
.

(ii)

Given two tori
i_{1}
and
i_{2}
both satisfying (
6.62
), then

\forall j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c},\hskip 10.00002pt\|\Delta_{12}r_{j}^{\infty}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

(6.67)

\forall j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c},\hskip 10.00002pt\|\Delta_{12}\mu_{j}^{\infty}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}|j|\|\Delta_{12}i\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.68)

Proof.

The proof consists in a KAM reduction algorithm. We may refer for instance to
[
4
,
8
,
29
,
37
,
47
]
to see some implementations of this method. Here, the main reference which fits with our purpose is
[
47
, Prop 6.5]
. Hence, we only explain here the main lines of this procedure and refer to this work for the technical details. In Proposition
6.3
, we managed to find the following reduction valid in the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0}),

\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}=\mathscr{L}_{0}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1},

where
\mathscr{L}_{0}
is an operator admitting the following structure

\mathscr{L}_{0}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{D}_{0}+\mathscr{R}_{0},

(6.69)

with
\mathscr{D}_{0}=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{D}_{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}=\big({\rm i}\mu_{j}^{0}(b,\omega)\big)_{j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}
a diagonal operator of pure imaginary spectrum and
\mathscr{R}_{0}=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{R}_{0}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}
a real and reversible Toeplitz in time operator of zero order. The proof consists in a recursive elimination of the remainder term
\mathscr{R}_{0}
. First notice that if we denote

\delta_{0}(s):=\gamma^{-1}\|\mathscr{R}_{0}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

then, in view of (
6.56
), we get

\displaystyle\delta_{0}(s)\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}\left(1+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

(6.70)

Hence, according to (
6.61
), (
6.62
) and the fact that
\sigma_{4}\geqslant\sigma_{3},
we infer

\displaystyle N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\delta_{0}(s_{h})

\displaystyle\leqslant CN_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon_{0}.

(6.71)

We construct recursively a sequence
(\mathscr{L}_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
of operators in the form

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{m}:=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{D}_{m}+\mathscr{R}_{m},

(6.72)

with
\mathscr{D}_{m}=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{D}_{m}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}=\big({\rm i}\mu_{j}^{m}(b,\omega)\big)_{j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}
a diagonal real reversible operator, that is,

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{m}\mathbf{e}_{l,j}={\rm i}\,\mu_{j}^{m}(b,\omega)\,\mathbf{e}_{l,j}\hskip 10.00002pt\hbox{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\mu_{-j}^{m}(b,\omega)=-\mu_{j}^{m}(b,\omega)

(6.73)

and
\mathscr{R}_{m}=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\mathscr{R}_{m}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}
a real and reversible Toeplitz in time operator of zero order quadratically smaller than the previous one in the Toeplitz topology introduced in Section
4.2
. To construct
\mathscr{D}_{m+1}
and
\mathscr{R}_{m+1},
we consider a linear invertible transformation close to the identity

\Phi_{m}=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\Psi_{m}:\mathcal{O}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(H^{s}\cap L^{2}_{\perp}),

where
\Psi_{m}
is small and depends on
\mathscr{R}_{m}
. Straightforward computations give

\displaystyle\Phi_{m}^{-1}\mathscr{L}_{m}\Phi_{m}

\displaystyle=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{D}_{m}+\Phi_{m}^{-1}\Big(\big[\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{D}_{m},\Psi_{m}\big]+P_{N_{m}}\mathscr{R}_{m}+P_{N_{m}}^{\perp}\mathscr{R}_{m}+\mathscr{R}_{m}\Psi_{m}\Big),

where the projector
P_{N_{m}}
was defined in (
4.16
). Therefore, we shall define
\Psi_{m}
so that it satisfies the following
homological equation

\big[\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{D}_{m},\Psi_{m}\big]+P_{N_{m}}\mathscr{R}_{m}=\lfloor P_{N_{m}}\mathscr{R}_{m}\rfloor,

(6.74)

where
\lfloor P_{N_{m}}\mathscr{R}_{m}\rfloor
is the diagonal part of the operator
P_{N_{m}}\mathscr{R}_{m}
. We emphasize that the notation
\lfloor\mathcal{R}\rfloor
with a general operator
\mathcal{R}
is defined as follows, for all
(l_{0},j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}
,

\mathcal{R}{\bf e}_{l_{0},j_{0}}=\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}\mathcal{R}_{l_{0},j_{0}}^{l,j}{\bf e}_{l,j}\Longrightarrow\lfloor\mathcal{R}\rfloor{\bf e}_{l_{0},j_{0}}=\mathcal{R}_{l_{0},j_{0}}^{l_{0},j_{0}}{\bf e}_{l_{0},j_{0}}=\big\langle\mathcal{R}{\bf e}_{l_{0},j_{0}},{\bf e}_{l_{0},j_{0}}\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1})}\,{\bf e}_{l_{0},j_{0}}.

(6.75)

Recall that we denote
{\bf e}_{l_{0},j_{0}}(\varphi,\theta)=e^{{\rm i}(l_{0}\cdot\varphi+j_{0}\theta)}
. The equation (
6.74
) can be solved using the Fourier decomposition of operators. Indeed, since
\mathscr{R}_{m}
is a real and reversible Toeplitz in time operator, then by virtue of Lemma
4.6
, we can write

(\mathscr{R}_{m})_{l_{0},j_{0}}^{l,j}:={\rm i}\,r_{j_{0},m}^{j}(b,\omega,l_{0}-l)\in{\rm i}\,\mathbb{R}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{ and }\hskip 10.00002pt(\mathscr{R}_{m})_{-l_{0},-j_{0}}^{-l,-j}=-(\mathscr{R}_{m})_{l_{0},j_{0}}^{l,j}.

(6.76)

Therefore, we define the operator
\Psi_{m}
by

(\Psi_{m})_{j_{0}}^{j}(b,\omega,l)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\varrho_{j_{0},m}^{j}(b,\omega,l)\,\,r_{j_{0},m}^{j}(b,\omega,l),&\mbox{if }\quad(l,j)\neq(0,j_{0})\\
0,&\mbox{if }\quad(l,j)=(0,j_{0}),\end{array}\right.

(6.77)

with

\displaystyle\varrho_{j_{0},m}^{j}(b,\omega,l):=\frac{\chi\left((\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{m}(b,\omega)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{m}(b,\omega))(\gamma\langle j-j_{0}\rangle)^{-1}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}\right)}{\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{m}(b,\omega)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{m}(b,\omega)},

(6.78)

where the cut-off function
\chi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R},[0,1])
is even and defined by

\chi(\xi)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\textnormal{if }|\xi|\leqslant\frac{1}{3}\\
1&\textnormal{if }|\xi|\geqslant\frac{1}{2}.\end{array}\right.

(6.79)

supplemented by the normal conditions

\forall\,l\,\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},\,\forall\,j\,\,\hbox{or}\,\,\,j_{0}\in\mathbb{S}_{0},\hskip 10.00002pt(\Psi_{m})_{j_{0}}^{j}(b,\omega,l)=0.

Using Lemma
4.1
-(v), we can prove that

\displaystyle\|\Psi_{m}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\gamma^{-1}\|P_{N_{m}}\mathscr{R}_{m}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s+\tau_{2}q+\tau_{2}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(6.80)

provided that

\displaystyle\forall\,(j,j_{0})\in(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2},\hskip 10.00002pt\max_{|\alpha|\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket}\sup_{(b,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}}\left|\partial_{b,\omega}^{\alpha}\left(\mu_{j}^{m}(b,\omega)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{m}(b,\omega)\right)\right|\leqslant C\,|j-j_{0}|.

(6.81)

Remark that (
6.81
) is satisfied for
m=0
in view of Lemma
3.3
-
(iv)
and (
6.20
). By construction,
\Psi_{m}=\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}\Psi_{m}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}
is a real and reversibility preserving Toeplitz in time operator well-defined in the whole set of parameters
\mathcal{O}
and solves the equation (
6.74
) when restricted to the Cantor set
\mathscr{O}_{m+1}^{\gamma}
defined recursively by
\mathscr{O}_{0}^{\gamma}=\mathcal{O}
and

\mathscr{O}_{m+1}^{\gamma}=\bigcap_{\underset{|l|\leqslant N_{m}}{(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times({\mathbb{S}}_{0}^{c})^{2}}\atop(l,j)\neq(0,j_{0})}\left\{(b,\omega)\in\mathscr{O}_{m}^{\gamma}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{s.t.}\hskip 10.00002pt|\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{m}(b,\omega)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{m}(b,\omega)|>\tfrac{\gamma\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}\right\}.

(6.82)

Consequently, in the Cantor set
\mathscr{O}_{m+1}^{\gamma},
one has

\mathscr{L}_{m+1}:=\Phi_{m}^{-1}\mathscr{L}_{m}\Phi_{m}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{D}_{m+1}+\mathscr{R}_{m+1},

(6.83)

with

\mathscr{D}_{m+1}=\mathscr{D}_{m}+\lfloor P_{N_{m}}\mathscr{R}_{m}\rfloor\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\mathscr{R}_{m+1}=\Phi_{m}^{-1}\big(-\Psi_{m}\,\lfloor P_{N_{m}}\mathscr{R}_{m}\rfloor+P_{N_{m}}^{\perp}\mathscr{R}_{m}+\mathscr{R}_{m}\Psi_{m}\big).

(6.84)

Remark that
\mathscr{D}_{m}
and
\lfloor P_{N_{m}}\mathscr{R}_{m}\rfloor
are Fourier multiplier Toeplitz operators that can be identified to their spectra
({\rm i}\mu_{j}^{m})_{j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}
and
({\rm i}r_{j}^{m})_{j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}
, namely

\displaystyle\forall(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c},\hskip 10.00002pt\mathscr{D}_{m}{\bf e}_{l,j}={\rm i}\mu_{j}^{m}\,{\bf e}_{l,j}\hskip 10.00002pt\hbox{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\lfloor P_{N_{m}}\mathscr{R}_{m}\rfloor\mathbf{e}_{l,j}={\rm i}r_{j}^{m}\,{\bf e}_{l,j}.

(6.85)

By construction, we find

\displaystyle\mu_{j}^{m+1}=\mu_{j}^{m}+r_{j}^{m}.

(6.86)

We set

\delta_{m}(s):=\gamma^{-1}\|\mathscr{R}_{m}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\widehat{\delta}_{m}(s):=\max\left(\gamma^{-1}\|\partial_{\theta}\mathscr{R}_{m}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\,,\delta_{m}(s)\right).

By Lemma
4.5
and (
6.80
), we get for all
S\geqslant\overline{s}\geqslant s\geqslant s_{0}
,

\displaystyle\delta_{m+1}(s)

\displaystyle\leqslant N_{m}^{s-\overline{s}}\delta_{m}(\overline{s})+CN_{m}^{\tau_{2}q+\tau_{2}}\delta_{m}(s_{0})\delta_{m}(s),

(6.87)

\displaystyle\widehat{\delta}_{m+1}(s)

\displaystyle\leqslant N_{m}^{s-\overline{s}}\widehat{\delta}_{m}(\overline{s})+CN_{m}^{\tau_{2}q+\tau_{2}+1}\widehat{\delta}_{m}(s_{0})\widehat{\delta}_{m}(s).

(6.88)

These recursive relations allow us to prove by induction on
m
that

\delta_{m}(\overline{s}_{l})\leqslant\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{-\mu_{2}}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{ and }\hskip 10.00002pt\delta_{m}(s_{h})\leqslant\left(2-\tfrac{1}{m+1}\right)\delta_{0}(s_{h}),

(6.89)

\displaystyle\widehat{\delta}_{m}(s_{0})\leqslant\widehat{\delta}_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{-\mu_{2}}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{ and }\hskip 10.00002pt\widehat{\delta}_{m}(s_{h})\leqslant\left(2-\tfrac{1}{m+1}\right)\widehat{\delta}_{0}(s_{h}),

(6.90)

with
\overline{s}_{l}
and
s_{h}
fixed by (
6.16
) and (
6.61
). Using the definition of the Fourier coefficients for an operator and the Toeplitz structure of
\mathscr{R}_{m}
, we find

\displaystyle\mu_{j}^{m+1}-\mu_{j}^{m}

\displaystyle=r_{j}^{m}

\displaystyle=-{\rm i}\langle P_{N_{m}}\mathscr{R}_{m}\mathbf{e}_{l,j},\mathbf{e}_{l,j}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1})}

\displaystyle=-{\rm i}\langle P_{N_{m}}\mathscr{R}_{m}\mathbf{e}_{0,j},\mathbf{e}_{0,j}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1})}.

By a duality argument combined with Lemma
4.5
, (
6.89
), (
6.70
) and (
6.62
), we infer

\displaystyle\|\mu_{j}^{m+1}-\mu_{j}^{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{-\mu_{2}}.

(6.91)

As the assumption (
6.81
) is satisfied with
\mathscr{D}_{m}
,
then we obtain by (
6.91
)

\displaystyle\forall\,(j,j_{0})\in(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2},\hskip 10.00002pt\max_{|\alpha|\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket}\sup_{(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}}\left|\partial_{\lambda,\omega}^{\alpha}\left(\mu_{j}^{m+1}(\lambda,\omega)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{m+1}(\lambda,\omega)\right)\right|\leqslant C\big(1+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1-q}N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{-\mu_{2}}\big)\,|j-j_{0}|.

Therefore, the sequence
\big(\mu_{j}^{m}\big)_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
converges in
W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},\mathbb{C}).
We denote
\mu_{j}^{\infty}
its limit and consider the associated diagonal operator
\mathscr{D}_{\infty}=\big({\rm i}\mu_{j}^{\infty}\big)_{j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}.

We introduce the sequence of operators

\left(\widehat{\Phi}_{m}\right)_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
by

\widehat{\Phi}_{0}:=\Phi_{0}\hskip 10.00002pt\hbox{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\hskip 10.00002pt\forall m\geqslant 1,\,\,\widehat{\Phi}_{m}:=\Phi_{0}\circ\Phi_{1}\circ...\circ\Phi_{m}.

(6.92)

It is obvious from the identity
{\Phi}_{m}=\hbox{Id}+\Psi_{m}
that

\widehat{\Phi}_{m+1}=\widehat{\Phi}_{m}+\widehat{\Phi}_{m}\Psi_{m+1}.

(6.93)

From (
6.93
), (
6.80
) and (
6.62
), we can make the sequence
(\widehat{\Phi}_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
converge to an element
\Phi_{\infty}
in the Toeplitz operator topology introduced in Section
4.2
. Moreover, arguing in a similar way to
[
47
]
we may prove that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Phi}_{m}^{-1}-\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\widehat{\Phi}_{m}-\Phi_{\infty}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m+1}^{-\mu_{2}}.

(6.94)

In addition, for any
m\in\mathbb{N},
we find in view of (
6.91
)

\displaystyle\|\mu_{j}^{\infty}-\mu_{j}^{m}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{n=m}^{\infty}\|\mu_{j}^{n+1}-\mu_{j}^{n}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\gamma\,\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{-\mu_{2}}.

(6.95)

Therefore, we deduce

\displaystyle\mu_{j}^{\infty}

\displaystyle=\mu_{j}^{0}+\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\big(\mu_{j}^{m+1}-\mu_{j}^{m}\big)

\displaystyle:=\mu_{j}^{0}+r_{j}^{\infty},

(6.96)

where
(\mu_{j}^{0})_{j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}
is described in Proposition
6.3
and takes the form

\mu_{j}^{0}(b,\omega,i_{0})=\Omega_{j}(b)+j\big(V_{i_{0}}^{\infty}(b,\omega)-\tfrac{1}{2}\big).

Define the diagonal operator
\mathscr{D}_{\infty}
by

\displaystyle\forall(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c},\hskip 10.00002pt\mathscr{D}_{\infty}{\bf e}_{l,j}={\rm i}\mu_{j}^{\infty}{\bf e}_{l,j}.

(6.97)

By the norm definition we obtain

\|\mathscr{D}_{m}-\mathscr{D}_{\infty}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}=\displaystyle\sup_{j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}\|\mu_{j}^{m}-\mu_{j}^{\infty}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

which gives by virtue of (
6.2.3
)

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{D}_{m}-\mathscr{D}_{\infty}\|_{\textnormal{\tiny{O-d}},q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C\,\gamma\,\delta_{0}(s_{h})N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{m}^{-\mu_{2}}.

(6.98)

Let us consider the following Cantor set for a given
n\in\mathbb{N},

\mathscr{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}(i_{0}):=\bigcap_{(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}\atop\underset{(l,j)\neq(0,j_{0})}{\langle l,j-j_{0}\rangle\leqslant N_{n}}}\Big\{(b,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{s.t.}\hskip 10.00002pt\big|\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty}(b,\omega)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty}(b,\omega)\big|>\tfrac{2\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}\Big\},

where the intermediate Cantor sets are defined in (
6.82
). A finite induction, in a similar way to
[
47
]
, allows to prove that

\mathscr{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}(i_{0})\subset\mathscr{O}_{n+1}^{\gamma},

Now define the operator

\mathscr{L}_{\infty}:=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{D}_{\infty}.

Writing

\mathscr{L}_{m}-\mathscr{L}_{\infty}=\mathscr{D}_{m}-\mathscr{D}_{\infty}+\mathscr{R}_{m},

then using (
6.98
) and (
6.89
), we can deduce the convergence of the sequence
(\mathscr{L}_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
to the operator
\mathscr{L}_{\infty}
in the Toeplitz operator topology. In view of (
6.92
) and (
6.83
) one obtains

\displaystyle\forall(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathscr{O}_{n+1}^{\gamma},\hskip 10.00002pt\widehat{\Phi}_{n}^{-1}\mathscr{L}_{0}\widehat{\Phi}_{n}

\displaystyle=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{D}_{n+1}+\mathscr{R}_{n+1}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{L}_{\infty}+\mathscr{D}_{n+1}-\mathscr{D}_{\infty}+\mathscr{R}_{n+1}.

It follows that for any
(\lambda,\omega)\in\mathscr{O}_{n+1}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\mathscr{L}_{0}\Phi_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{L}_{\infty}+\mathscr{D}_{n+1}-\mathscr{D}_{\infty}+\mathscr{R}_{n+1}

\displaystyle+\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\mathscr{L}_{0}\left(\Phi_{\infty}-\widehat{\Phi}_{n}\right)+\left(\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}-\widehat{\Phi}_{n}^{-1}\right)\mathscr{L}_{0}\widehat{\Phi}_{n}

\displaystyle:=\mathscr{L}_{\infty}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{2}.

According to Lemma
4.5
, (
6.58
), (
6.2.3
), (
6.62
) and (
6.94
) we get (
6.66
).
Next let us see how to get the estimate (
6.65
).
Recall that

r_{j}^{\infty}=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}r_{j}^{m}\hskip 10.00002pt\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{with}\hskip 10.00002pt\hskip 10.00002ptr_{j}^{m}=-{\rm i}\big\langle P_{N_{m}}\mathscr{R}_{m}\mathbf{e}_{0,j},\mathbf{e}_{0,j}\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1})}.

An integration by parts gives

\displaystyle\big\langle P_{N_{m}}\mathscr{R}_{m}\mathbf{e}_{0,j},\mathbf{e}_{0,j}\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1})}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{{\rm i}}{j}\big\langle P_{N_{m}}\mathscr{R}_{m}\mathbf{e}_{0,j},\partial_{\theta}\mathbf{e}_{0,j}\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1})}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{-{\rm i}}{j}\big\langle P_{N_{m}}\partial_{\theta}\mathscr{R}_{m}\mathbf{e}_{0,j},\mathbf{e}_{0,j}\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{d+1})}.

Using a duality argument
H^{s_{0}}-H^{-s_{0}}
combined with Lemma
4.5
, (
6.90
), (
6.56
) and (
6.62
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|r_{j}^{\infty}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\displaystyle|j|^{-1}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}.

The difference estimates (
6.67
) and (
6.68
) can be obtained by fixing
\mathtt{p}=4\tau_{2}q+4\tau_{2}
as in
[
47
]
. The proof of Proposition
6.4
is now complete.
∎

6.3
Construction and tame estimates for the approximate inverse

At this step, we can construct an almost approximate right inverse for
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}
defined in (
6.7
). This enables to find in turn an almost approximate right inverse for the whole operator
d_{i,\alpha}\mathcal{F}(i_{0},\alpha_{0})
given by (
6.1
).

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
(\gamma,q,d,\tau_{1},s_{0},s_{h},\mu_{2},S)
satisfying (
1.11
), (
1.10
) and (
6.61
).
There exists
\sigma:=\sigma(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},q,d)\geqslant\sigma_{4}
such that if

\varepsilon\gamma^{-2-q}N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{h}+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant 1,

(6.99)

then, the following assertions hold true.

(i)

Consider the operator
\mathscr{L}_{\infty}
defined in Proposition
\ref{reduction of the remainder term},
then there exists a family of linear reversible operators
\big(\mathtt{T}_{n}\big)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
defined in
\mathcal{O}
satisfying the estimate

\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\hskip 10.00002pt\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\|\mathtt{T}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\|\rho\|_{q,s+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

and such that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, in the Cantor set

\Lambda_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})=\bigcap_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}\atop|l|\leqslant N_{n}}\left\{(b,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{s.t.}\hskip 10.00002pt\left|\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty}(b,\omega,i_{0})\right|>\tfrac{\gamma\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\right\},

we have

\mathscr{L}_{\infty}\mathtt{T}_{n}=\textnormal{Id}+\mathtt{E}^{3}_{n},

with

\forall s_{0}\leqslant s\leqslant\overline{s}\leqslant S,\hskip 10.00002pt\|\mathtt{E}^{3}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim N_{n}^{s-\overline{s}}\gamma^{-1}\|\rho\|_{q,\overline{s}+1+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(ii)

There exists a family of linear reversible operators
\big(\mathtt{T}_{\omega,n}\big)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfying

\forall\,s\in\,[s_{0},S],\hskip 10.00002pt\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\|\mathtt{T}_{\omega,n}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\left(\|\rho\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

(6.100)

and such that in the Cantor set

\mathcal{G}_{n}(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2},i_{0}):=\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})\cap\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}(i_{0})\cap\Lambda_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0}),

(6.101)

we have

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}\mathtt{T}_{\omega,n}=\textnormal{Id}+\mathtt{E}_{n},

where
\mathtt{E}_{n}
satisfies the following estimate

\displaystyle\forall\,s\in[s_{0},S],\hskip 10.00002pt

\displaystyle\|\mathtt{E}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim N_{n}^{s_{0}-s}\gamma^{-1}\Big(\|\rho\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 10.00002pt+\varepsilon\gamma^{-3}N_{0}^{{\mu}_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.102)

Recall that
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega},

\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
and
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}(i_{0})
are given by (
6.7
) and Propositions
6.2
and
6.4
, respectively.

(iii)

In the Cantor set
\mathcal{G}_{n}(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2},i_{0})
, we have the following splitting

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}=\widehat{\mathtt{L}}_{\omega,n}+\widehat{\mathtt{R}}_{n}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{with}\hskip 10.00002pt\widehat{\mathtt{L}}_{\omega,n}\mathtt{T}_{\omega,n}=\textnormal{Id}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\widehat{\mathtt{R}}_{n}=\mathtt{E}_{n}\widehat{\mathtt{L}}_{\omega,n},

where
\widehat{\mathtt{L}}_{\omega,n}
and
\widehat{\mathtt{R}}_{n}
are reversible operators defined in
\mathcal{O}
and satisfy the following estimates

\displaystyle\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\hskip 10.00002pt

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\|\widehat{\mathtt{L}}_{\omega,n}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\|\rho\|_{q,s+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

(6.103)

\displaystyle\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\hskip 10.00002pt

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathtt{R}}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim N_{n}^{s_{0}-s}\gamma^{-1}\left(\|\rho\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 10.00002pt+\varepsilon\gamma^{-3}N_{0}^{\mu_{2}}N_{n+1}^{-\mu_{2}}\|\rho\|_{q,s_{0}+\sigma}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.104)

Proof.

(i)
First recall from Proposition
6.4
that

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{D}_{\infty}.

Using the projectors defined in (
4.1
), we can split this operator as follows

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{N_{n}}\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{N_{n}}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}+\mathscr{D}_{\infty}-\Pi_{N_{n}}^{\perp}\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{N_{n}}^{\perp}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}

\displaystyle:=\mathtt{L}_{n}-\mathtt{R}_{n},

(6.105)

where

\mathtt{R}_{n}:=\Pi_{N_{n}}^{\perp}\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\Pi_{N_{n}}^{\perp}\Pi_{\mathbb{S}_{0}}^{\perp}.

According to the structure of
\mathscr{D}_{\infty}
in Proposition
6.4
, we obtain from (
6.3
),

\forall(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c},\hskip 10.00002pt{\bf e}_{-l,-j}\mathtt{L}_{n}{\bf e}_{l,j}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\rm i}\big(\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty}\big)&\hbox{if }|l|\leqslant N_{n}\\
{\rm i}\,\mu_{j}^{\infty}&\hbox{if }|l|>N_{n}.\end{array}\right.

Let us now consider the diagonal operator
\mathtt{T}_{n}
defined by

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{n}\rho(b,\omega,\varphi,\theta):=

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}\atop|l|\leqslant N_{n}}\tfrac{\chi\left((\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty}(b,\omega,i_{0}))\gamma^{-1}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}\right)}{\omega\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty}(b,\omega,i_{0})}\rho_{l,j}(b,\omega)\,e^{{\rm i}(l\cdot\varphi+j\theta)}

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}\atop|l|>N_{n}}\tfrac{\rho_{l,j}(b,\omega)}{\mu_{j}^{\infty}(b,\omega,i_{0})}\,e^{{\rm i}(l\cdot\varphi+j\theta)},

where
\chi
is the cut-off function introduced in (
6.79
) and
\left(\rho_{l,j}(b,\omega)\right)_{l,j}
are the Fourier coefficients of
\rho
. Now recall the expansion of the perturbed eigenvalues given by Proposition
6.4
, namely

\mu_{j}^{\infty}(b,\omega,i_{0})=\Omega_{j}(b)+jr^{1}(b,\omega)+r_{j}^{\infty}(b,\omega)\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{ with }\hskip 10.00002ptr^{1}(b,\omega)=V_{i_{0}}^{\infty}(b,\omega)-\tfrac{1}{2}.

In view of Lemma
3.3
-(iv), (
6.54
) and (
6.65
), they satisfy the following estimates

\forall j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c},\hskip 10.00002pt\|\mu^{\infty}_{j}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim|j|.

According to Lemma
3.3
-(ii), (
6.54
), (
6.65
) and the smallness condition (
6.99
) we infer

|j|\lesssim\|\mu^{\infty}_{j}\|_{0}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\|\mu^{\infty}_{j}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Computations based on Lemma
4.1
-(vi) give

\displaystyle\forall s\geqslant s_{0},\hskip 10.00002pt\|\mathtt{T}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\|\rho\|_{q,s+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.106)

In addition, by construction

\mathtt{L}_{n}\mathtt{T}_{n}=\textnormal{Id}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{in }\Lambda_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})

(6.107)

since
\chi(\cdot)=1
in this set. Gathering (
6.107
) and (
6.3
) yields

\displaystyle\forall\,(b,\omega)\in\Lambda_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0}),\hskip 10.00002pt\mathscr{L}_{\infty}\mathtt{T}_{n}

\displaystyle=\textnormal{Id}-\mathtt{R}_{n}\mathtt{T}_{n}

\displaystyle:=\textnormal{Id}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{3}.

(6.108)

Remark that by Lemma
4.1
-(ii),

\forall\,s_{0}\leqslant s\leqslant\overline{s}\leqslant S,\hskip 10.00002pt\|\mathtt{R}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim N_{n}^{s-\overline{s}}\|\rho\|_{q,\overline{s}+1}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Putting this estimate with (
6.106
) implies

\displaystyle\forall\,s_{0}\leqslant s\leqslant\overline{s}\leqslant S,\hskip 10.00002pt\|\mathtt{E}^{3}_{n}\rho\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{n}^{s-\overline{s}}\gamma^{-1}\|\rho\|_{q,\overline{s}+1+\tau_{1}q+\tau_{1}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

(6.109)

(ii)
We set

\displaystyle\mathtt{T}_{\omega,n}:=\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\Phi_{\infty}\mathtt{T}_{n}\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1},

(6.110)

where the operators
\mathscr{B}_{\perp}
and
\Phi_{\infty}
are defined in Propositions
6.3
and
6.4
respectively. Notice that
\mathtt{T}_{\omega,n}
is defined in the whole range of parameters
\mathcal{O}.
Since the condition (
6.99
) is satisfied, then, both Propositions
6.2
and
6.4
apply and the estimate (
6.100
) is obtained combining (
6.52
), (
6.63
), (
6.106
) and (
6.99
).
Now combining Propositions
6.3
and
6.4
, we find that in the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})\cap\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}(i_{0})

the following decomposition holds

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}_{\infty}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\Phi_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\Phi^{-1}_{\infty}\mathscr{L}_{0}\Phi_{\infty}+\Phi^{-1}_{\infty}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}\Phi_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{L}_{\infty}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{2}+\Phi^{-1}_{\infty}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}\Phi_{\infty}.

According to (
6.3
), one finds that in the Cantor set
\mathcal{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})\cap\mathscr{O}_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{2}}(i_{0})\cap\Lambda_{\infty,n}^{\gamma,\tau_{1}}(i_{0})
the following identity holds

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}_{\infty}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\Phi_{\infty}\mathtt{T}_{n}

\displaystyle=\textnormal{Id}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{3}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{2}\mathtt{T}_{n}+\Phi^{-1}_{\infty}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}\Phi_{\infty}\mathtt{T}_{n},

which implies in turn, in view of (
6.110
), the following identity in
\mathcal{G}_{n}(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2},i_{0})

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}\mathtt{T}_{\omega,n}

\displaystyle=\textnormal{Id}+\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\Phi_{\infty}\big(\mathtt{E}_{n}^{3}+\mathtt{E}_{n}^{2}\mathtt{T}_{n}+\Phi^{-1}_{\infty}\mathtt{E}_{n}^{1}\Phi_{\infty}\mathtt{T}_{n}\big)\Phi^{-1}_{\infty}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}

\displaystyle:=\textnormal{Id}+\mathtt{E}_{n}.

(6.111)

Combining (
6.3
), (
6.55
), (
6.66
), (
6.109
), (
6.106
), (
6.52
), (
6.63
) and (
6.99
), we get (
(ii)
), up to take
\sigma
large enough.

(iii)
By virtue of (
6.3
), one can write in the Cantor set
\mathcal{G}_{n}(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2},i_{0})

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}=\mathtt{T}_{\omega,n}^{-1}+\mathtt{E}_{n}\mathtt{T}_{\omega,n}^{-1}.

(6.112)

Putting together (
6.110
) and (
6.107
), one finds in the Cantor set
\mathcal{G}_{n}(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2},i_{0})

\widehat{\mathtt{L}}_{\omega,n}:=\mathtt{T}_{\omega,n}^{-1}=\mathscr{B}_{\perp}\Phi_{\infty}\mathtt{L}_{n}\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\mathscr{B}_{\perp}^{-1}.

Therefore, (
6.112
) can be rewritten

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\omega}=\widehat{\mathtt{L}}_{\omega,n}+\widehat{\mathtt{R}}_{n}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{with}\hskip 10.00002pt\widehat{\mathtt{R}}_{n}:=\mathtt{E}_{n}\widehat{\mathtt{L}}_{\omega,n}.

The estimate (
6.103
) is obtained gathering (
6.3
), (
6.52
), (
6.63
) and (
6.99
). Finally, (
6.103
) together with (
(ii)
) implies (
6.104
). This ends the proof of Proposition
6.5
.
∎

The following theorem, which can be found in
[
11
,
16
,
37
]
, states that the linearized operator
d_{i,\alpha}{\mathcal{F}}(i_{0},\alpha_{0})
in (
6.1
) admits an approximate right inverse on a suitable Cantor set.

Theorem 6.1
.

(Approximate inverse)

Let
(\gamma,q,d,\tau_{1},\tau_{2},s_{0},s_{h},\mu_{2})
satisfy (
1.11
), (
1.10
), (
6.16
) and (
6.61
).
Then there exists
\overline{\sigma}=\overline{\sigma}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},d,q)>0
and a family of reversible operators
{\rm T}_{0}:={\rm T}_{0,n}(i_{0})
such that if the smallness condition (
6.99
) holds, then
for all
g=(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})
, satisfying

g_{1}(\varphi)=g_{1}(\varphi),\hskip 10.00002ptg_{2}(-\varphi)=-g_{2}(\varphi)\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002ptg_{3}(-\varphi)=(\mathscr{S}g_{3})(\varphi),

the function
{\rm T}_{0}g
satisfies the following estimate

\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\hskip 10.00002pt\|{\rm T}_{0}g\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\left(\|g\|_{q,s+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|g\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\right).

Moreover
{\rm T}_{0}
is an almost-approximate right inverse of
d_{i,\alpha}\mathcal{F}(i_{0},\alpha_{0})
in the Cantor set
\mathcal{G}_{n}(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2},i_{0})
defined by (
6.101
). More precisely,

\forall(b,\omega)\in\mathcal{G}_{n}(\gamma,\tau_{1},\tau_{2},i_{0}),\hskip 10.00002ptd_{i,\alpha}\mathcal{F}(i_{0})\circ{\rm T}_{0}-{\rm Id}=\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{1}+\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{2}+\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{3},

where the operators
\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{1}
,
\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{2}
and
\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{3}
are defined in the whole set
\mathcal{O}
with the estimates

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{1}g\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{-1}\|\mathcal{F}(i_{0},\alpha_{0})\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|g\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}},

\displaystyle\forall b\geqslant 0,\hskip 10.00002pt\|\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{2}g\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{-b}\big(\|g\|_{q,s_{0}+b+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+\varepsilon\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{0}+b+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big\|g\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\big)\,,

\displaystyle\forall b\in[0,S],\hskip 10.00002pt\|\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{3}g\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{n}^{-b}\gamma^{-2}\Big(\|g\|_{q,s_{0}+b+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}+{\varepsilon\gamma^{-2}}\|\mathfrak{I}_{0}\|_{q,s_{0}+b+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\|g\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\varepsilon\gamma^{-4}N_{0}^{{\mu}_{2}}{N_{n}^{-\mu_{2}}}\|g\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

7
Nash-Moser iteration and measure of the final Cantor set

In this last section, we shall find a non-trivial solution
(b,\omega)\mapsto(i_{\infty}(b,\omega),\alpha_{\infty}(b,\omega))
to the equation

\mathcal{F}(i,\alpha,b,\omega,\varepsilon)=0,

where
\mathcal{F}
is the functional defined in (
5.19
). This done by using a Nash-Moser scheme in a similar way to the series of papers
[
3
,
16
,
37
,
47
]
. The solutions are constructed for parameters
(b,\omega)
belonging to the intersection of all the Cantor sets
\mathcal{G}_{\infty}^{\gamma}
on which we are able to invert the linearized operator at the different steps. In order to find a solution to the original problem, we must rigidify the frequencies
\omega
so that it coincides with the equilibrium frequencies. This amounts to consider a frequency curve
b\mapsto\omega(b,\varepsilon)
implicitly defined by the equation

\alpha_{\infty}\big(b,\omega(b,\varepsilon)\big)=-\omega_{\textnormal{Eq}}(b).

Considering the associated rigidified Cantor set

\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}=\Big\{b\in(b_{0},b_{1})\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{s.t.}\hskip 10.00002pt\big(b,\omega(b,\varepsilon)\big)\in\mathcal{G}_{\infty}^{\gamma}\Big\},

we have a solution to the original problem provided that the measure of
\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}
is non-zero. This will be checked, in Section
7.2
, by perturbative arguments in the spirit of the previous works
[
3
,
4
,
16
,
37
,
47
]
. This proves in particular Theorem
1.1
.

7.1
Nash-Moser iteration

In this section we implement the Nash-Moser scheme, which is a modified Newton method consisting in a recursive construction of approximate solutions of the equation
\mathcal{F}\big(i,\alpha,b,\omega\big):=\mathcal{F}\big(i,\alpha,b,\omega,\varepsilon\big)=0
where the functional
\mathcal{F}
is defined in (
5.19
). At each step of this procedure, we need to construct an approximate inverse of the linearized operator at a state near the equilibrium by applying the reduction procedure developed in Section
6
. This allows to get Theorem
6.1
with the suitable tame estimates associated to the final loss of regularity
\overline{\sigma}
that could be arranged to be large enough. We point out that
\overline{\sigma}
depends only on the shape of the Cantor set through the parameters
\tau_{1},\tau_{2},d
and
q
but it is independent of the regularity of the solutions that we want to construct. Now, we shall fix the following parameters needed to implement the Nash-Moser scheme and related to the loss of regularity
\overline{\sigma}.

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}\overline{a}&=&\tau_{2}+2\\
\mu_{1}&=&3q(\tau_{2}+2)+6\overline{\sigma}+6\\
a_{1}&=&6q(\tau_{2}+2)+12\overline{\sigma}+15\\
a_{2}&=&3q(\tau_{2}+2)+6\overline{\sigma}+9\\
\mu_{2}&=&2q(\tau_{2}+2)+5\overline{\sigma}+7\\
s_{h}&=&s_{0}+4q(\tau_{2}+2)+9\overline{\sigma}+11\\
b_{1}&=&2s_{h}-s_{0}.\end{array}\right.

(7.1)

We shall now impose the constraint relating
\gamma
and
N_{0}
to
\varepsilon

0<a<\tfrac{1}{\mu_{2}+q+2},\hskip 10.00002pt\gamma:=\varepsilon^{a},\hskip 10.00002ptN_{0}:=\gamma^{-1}.

(7.2)

We consider the finite dimensional subspaces

\displaystyle E_{n}:=

\displaystyle\Big\{\mathfrak{I}=(\Theta,I,z)\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{s.t.}\hskip 10.00002pt\Theta=\Pi_{n}\Theta,\hskip 10.00002ptI=\Pi_{n}I\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002ptz=\Pi_{n}z\Big\},

where
\Pi_{n}
is the projector defined by

f(\varphi,\theta)=\sum_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}}f_{l,j}e^{{\rm i}(l\cdot\varphi+j\theta)}\hskip 10.00002pt\Rightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\Pi_{n}f(\varphi,\theta)=\sum_{\langle l,j\rangle\leqslant N_{n}}f_{l,j}e^{{\rm i}(l\cdot\varphi+j\theta)}.

The main result of this section can be stated as follows. The proof is now standard and to avoid the technical details we refer to the works
[
16
,
37
,
47
]
. In our context, we may follow closely the version detailed in
[
47
, Prop. 7.1]
. In particular the roles of the parameters in (
7.1
) are explained there.

Proposition 7.1
.

(Nash-Moser)

Let
(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},q,d,s_{0})
satisfy (
1.11
) and (
1.10
). Consider the parameters fixed by (
7.1
) and (
7.2
).
There exist
C_{\ast}>0
and
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in[0,\varepsilon_{0}]
we get for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
the following properties.

(\mathcal{P}1)_{n}

There exists a
q
-times differentiable function

W_{n}:\begin{array}[t]{rcl}\mathcal{O}&\rightarrow&E_{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d+1}\\
(b,\omega)&\mapsto&\big(\mathfrak{I}_{n},\alpha_{n}-\omega,0\big)\end{array}

satisfying

W_{0}=0\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{ and }\hskip 10.00002pt\hbox{for}\hskip 10.00002ptn\in\mathbb{N}^{*},\hskip 10.00002pt\,\|W_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}.

By setting

U_{0}=\Big((\varphi,0,0),\omega,(b,\omega)\Big)\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\hbox{for}\hskip 10.00002ptn\in\mathbb{N}^{*},\hskip 10.00002ptU_{n}=U_{0}+W_{n}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002ptH_{n}=U_{n}-U_{n-1},

(7.3)

then

\forall s\in[s_{0},S],\,\|H_{1}\|_{q,s}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{ and }\hskip 10.00002pt\forall\,2\leqslant k\leqslant n,\,\|H_{k}\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{k-1}^{-a_{2}}.

(7.4)

We also have for
n\geqslant 2,

\|H_{n}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}.

(7.5)

(\mathcal{P}2)_{n}

Define

i_{n}=(\varphi,0,0)+\mathfrak{I}_{n},\hskip 10.00002pt\gamma_{n}=\gamma(1+2^{-n}),

(7.6)

then
i_{n}
satisfies the following reversibility condition

\mathfrak{S}i_{n}(\varphi)=i_{n}(-\varphi),

(7.7)

where
\mathfrak{S}
is defined by (
5.10
). Define also

\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\gamma}=\mathcal{O}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{ and }\hskip 10.00002pt\mathcal{A}_{n+1}^{\gamma}=\mathcal{A}_{n}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{G}_{n}(\gamma_{n+1},\tau_{1},\tau_{2},i_{n})

where
\mathcal{G}_{n}(\gamma_{n+1},\tau_{1},\tau_{2},i_{n})
is defined in Proposition
\ref{inversion of the linearized operator in the normal directions}.
Consider the open sets

\forall\mathtt{r}>0,\hskip 10.00002pt\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\mathtt{r}}:=\Big\{(b,\omega)\in\mathcal{O}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{s.t.}\hskip 10.00002pt{\mathtt{dist}}\big((b,\omega),\mathcal{A}_{n}^{\gamma}\big)<\mathtt{r}N_{n}^{-\overline{a}}\Big\}

where
\displaystyle{\mathtt{dist}}(x,A)=\inf_{y\in A}\|x-y\|
. Then we have the following estimate

\|\mathcal{F}(U_{n})\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathrm{O}_{n}^{\gamma}}\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon N_{n-1}^{-a_{1}}.

(\mathcal{P}3)_{n}

\|W_{n}\|_{q,b_{1}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{\mu_{1}}.

A non trivial reversible quasi-periodic solution of our problem is obtained as the limit of the sequence
(U_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
according to the fast convergence stated in Proposition
7.1
. This is explained in the following corollary.

Corollary 7.1
.

There exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that, for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}),
the following assertions hold true. We consider the Cantor set
\mathcal{G}_{\infty}^{\gamma}
, related to
\varepsilon
through
\gamma
, and defined by

\mathcal{G}_{\infty}^{\gamma}:=\bigcap_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{A}_{n}^{\gamma}.

There exists a function

U_{\infty}:\begin{array}[t]{rcl}\mathcal{O}&\rightarrow&\left(\mathbb{T}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times L^{2}_{\perp}\cap H^{s_{0}}\right)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d+1}\\
(b,\omega)&\mapsto&\big(i_{\infty}(b,\omega),\alpha_{\infty}(b,\omega),(b,\omega)\big)\end{array}

such that

\forall(b,\omega)\in\mathcal{G}_{\infty}^{\gamma},\hskip 10.00002pt\mathcal{F}(U_{\infty}(b,\omega))=0.

In addition,
i_{\infty}
is reversible and
\alpha_{\infty}\in W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},\mathbb{R}^{d})
with

\alpha_{\infty}(b,\omega)=\omega+\mathrm{r}_{\varepsilon}(b,\omega)\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{ and }\hskip 10.00002pt\|\mathrm{r}_{\varepsilon}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}.

(7.8)

Moreover, there exists a
q
-times differentiable function
b\in(b_{0},b_{1})\mapsto\omega(b,\varepsilon)
with

\omega(b,\varepsilon)=-\omega_{\textnormal{Eq}}(b)+\bar{r}_{\varepsilon}(b),\hskip 10.00002pt\|\bar{r}_{\varepsilon}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}},

(7.9)

and

\forall b\in\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon},\hskip 10.00002pt\mathcal{F}\big(U_{\infty}(b,\omega(b,\varepsilon))\big)=0\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\alpha_{\infty}\big(b,\omega(b,\varepsilon)\big)=-\omega_{\textnormal{Eq}}(b),

where the Cantor set
\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}
is defined by

\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}=\Big\{b\in(b_{0},b_{1})\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{s.t.}\hskip 10.00002pt\big(b,\omega(b,\varepsilon)\big)\in\mathcal{G}_{\infty}^{\gamma}\Big\}.

(7.10)

Proof.

In view of (
7.3
) and (
7.4
), we obtain

\|W_{n+1}-W_{n}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}=\|H_{n+1}\|_{q,s_{0}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant\|H_{n+1}\|_{q,s_{0}+\overline{\sigma}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n}^{-a_{2}}.

This implies the convergence of the sequence
\left(W_{n}\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}.
Its limit is denoted by

W_{\infty}:=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}W_{n}:=(\mathfrak{I}_{\infty},\alpha_{\infty}-\omega,0,0)

and we set

U_{\infty}:=\big(i_{\infty},\alpha_{\infty},(b,\omega)\big)=U_{0}+W_{\infty}.

Taking
n\to\infty
in (
7.7
) gives

\mathfrak{S}i_{\infty}(\varphi)=i_{\infty}(-\varphi).

According to Proposition
7.1
-
(\mathcal{P}2)_{n}
, we get for small values of
\varepsilon

\forall(b,\omega)\in\mathcal{G}_{\infty}^{\gamma},\hskip 10.00002pt\mathcal{F}\Big(i_{\infty}(b,\omega),\alpha_{\infty}(b,\omega),(b,\omega),\varepsilon\Big)=0,

(7.11)

where
\mathcal{F}
is the functional defined in (
5.19
). We emphasize that the Cantor set
\mathcal{G}_{\infty}^{\gamma}
depends on
\varepsilon
through
\gamma
fixed in (
7.2
). Now, from Proposition
7.1
-
(\mathcal{P}1)_{n}
, we deduce that

\alpha_{\infty}(b,\omega)=\omega+\mathrm{r}_{\varepsilon}(b,\omega)\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{ with }\hskip 10.00002pt\|\mathrm{r}_{\varepsilon}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}.

Next we shall prove the second result and check the existence of solutions to the original Hamiltonian equation. First recall that the open set
\mathcal{O}
is defined in (
1.9
) by

\mathcal{O}=(b_{0},b_{1})\times\mathscr{U}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{with}\hskip 10.00002pt\mathscr{U}=B(0,R_{0})\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{for some large }R_{0}>0,

where the ball
\mathscr{U}
is taken to contain the equilibrium frequency vector
b\mapsto{\omega}_{\textnormal{Eq}}(b).
In view of (
7.8
), we obtain that for any
b\in(b_{0},b_{1}),
the mapping
\omega\mapsto\alpha_{\infty}(b,\omega)
is invertible from
\mathscr{U}
into its image
\alpha_{\infty}(b,\mathscr{U})
and we have

\widehat{\omega}=\alpha_{\infty}(b,\omega)=\omega+\mathrm{r}_{\varepsilon}(b,\omega)\Leftrightarrow\omega=\alpha_{\infty}^{-1}(b,\widehat{\omega})=\widehat{\omega}+\widehat{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}(b,\widehat{\omega}).

In particular,

\widehat{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}(b,\widehat{\omega})=-\mathrm{r}_{\varepsilon}(b,\omega).

Differentiating the previous relation and using (
7.8
), we find

\|\widehat{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}.

(7.12)

Now, we set

{\omega}(b,\varepsilon):=\alpha_{\infty}^{-1}(b,-{\omega}_{\textnormal{Eq}}(b))=-{\omega}_{\textnormal{Eq}}(b)+\overline{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}(b)\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{ with }\hskip 10.00002pt\overline{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}(b):=\widehat{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}\big(b,-{\omega}_{\textnormal{Eq}}(b)\big)

and consider the following Cantor set

\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}:=\Big\{b\in(b_{0},b_{1})\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{s.t.}\hskip 10.00002pt\big(b,\omega(b,\varepsilon)\big)\in\mathcal{G}_{\infty}^{\gamma}\Big\}.

Then, according to (
7.11
), we get

\forall b\in\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon},\hskip 10.00002pt\mathcal{F}\Big(U_{\infty}\big(b,\omega(b,\varepsilon)\big)\Big)=0.

This gives a nontrivial reversible solution for the original Hamiltonian equation provided that
b\in\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}.

From Lemma
3.3
, we obtain that all the derivatives up to order
q
of
\omega_{\textnormal{Eq}}
are uniformly bounded on
[b_{0},b_{1}].
As a consequence, the chain rule and (
7.12
) imply

\|\overline{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\|\omega(\cdot,\varepsilon)\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\lesssim 1+\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}\lesssim 1.

(7.13)

This achieves the proof of Corollary
7.1
.
∎

7.2
Measure estimates

In this last section, we check that the Cantor set
\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon},
defined in (
7.10
), of parameters generating non-trivial quasi-periodic solutions is non trivial. More precisely, we have the following proposition giving a lower bound measure for
\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
q_{0}
be defined as in Lemma
3.5
and impose (
7.1
) and (
7.2
) with
q=q_{0}+1.
Assume the additional conditions

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\tau_{1}>dq_{0}\\
\tau_{2}>\tau_{1}+dq_{0}\\
\upsilon=\frac{1}{q_{0}+3}.\end{array}\right.

(7.14)

Then there exists
C>0
such that

\big|\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}\big|\geqslant(b_{1}-b_{0})-C\varepsilon^{\frac{a\upsilon}{q_{0}}}.

In particular,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\big|\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}\big|=b_{1}-b_{0}.

Remark 7.1
.

The constraints listed in (
7.14
) appear naturally in the proof, see (
7.22
) and (
7.27
), for the convergence of series and for smallness conditions. Notice that these conditions agree with (
1.10
) and Proposition
6.2
.

Proof.

According to Corollary
7.1
, we can decompose the Cantor set
\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}
in the following intersection

\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}:=\bigcap_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{ where }\hskip 10.00002pt\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}:=\Big\{b\in(b_{0},b_{1})\hskip 10.00002pt\hbox{s.t}\hskip 10.00002pt\big(b,{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\big)\in\mathcal{A}_{n}^{\gamma}\Big\}.

(7.15)

Recall that the intermediate sets
\mathcal{A}_{n}^{\gamma}
and the perturbed frequency vector
\omega(b,\varepsilon)
are respectively defined in Proposition
7.1
and in (
7.8
). Instead of measuring directly
\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}
, we rather estimate the measure of its complementary set in
(b_{0},b_{1}).
Thus, we write

(b_{0},b_{1})\setminus\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}=\big((b_{0},b_{1})\setminus\mathcal{C}_{0}^{\varepsilon}\big)\sqcup\bigsqcup_{n=0}^{\infty}\big(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}\big).

(7.16)

Then, we have to measure all the sets appearing in the decomposition (
7.16
). This can be done by using Lemma
3.6
together with some trivial inclusions allowing to link the time and space Fourier modes in order to make the series converge. For more details, we refer to Lemmata
7.1
,
7.2
and
7.3
.

From (
7.2
) and (
7.9
), one obtains

\sup_{b\in(b_{0},b_{1})}\left|\omega(b,\varepsilon)+\omega_{\textnormal{Eq}}(b)\right|\leqslant\|\overline{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}=C\varepsilon^{1-a(1+q\overline{a})}.

Notice that the conditions (
7.1
) and (
7.2
) imply in particular

0<a<\frac{1}{1+q\overline{a}}.

Therefore, taking
\varepsilon
small enough yields

\sup_{b\in(b_{0},b_{1})}\left|\omega(b,\varepsilon)+\omega_{\textnormal{Eq}}(b)\right|\leqslant\|\overline{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}\leqslant 1.

Recall that
\mathscr{U}=B(0,R_{0})
, then, up to take
R_{0}
large enough, we get

\forall b\in(b_{0},b_{1}),\;\forall\varepsilon\in[0,\varepsilon_{0}),\hskip 10.00002pt\omega(b,\varepsilon)\in\mathscr{U}=B(0,R_{0}).

Recall that
\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\gamma}=\mathcal{O}=(b_{0},b_{1})\times\mathscr{U}
then, from (
7.15
),

\mathcal{C}_{0}^{\varepsilon}=(b_{0},b_{1})

and coming back to (
7.16
), we find

\displaystyle\Big|(b_{0},b_{1})\setminus\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}\Big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Big|\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}\Big|

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{S}_{n}.

(7.17)

In accordance with the notations used in Propositions
6.3
and
6.4
, we denote the perturbed frequencies associated with the reduced linearized operator at state
i_{n}
in the following way

\displaystyle\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle:=\mu_{j}^{\infty}\big(b,{\omega}(b,\varepsilon),i_{n}\big)

\displaystyle=\Omega_{j}(b)+jr^{1,n}(b,\varepsilon)+r_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon),

(7.18)

where

\displaystyle r^{1,n}(b,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle:=V_{n}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon)-\tfrac{1}{2},

\displaystyle V_{n}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle:=V_{i_{n}}^{\infty}(b,{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)),

\displaystyle r_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle:=r_{j}^{\infty}\big(b,{\omega}(b,\varepsilon),i_{n}\big).

Now, according to (
7.15
), Propositions
6.4
,
6.5
and
6.2
one can write for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}=\bigcup_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{(0,0)\}\atop|l|\leqslant N_{n}}\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\bigcup_{(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}\atop|l|\leqslant N_{n}}\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})\bigcup_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}\atop|l|\leqslant N_{n}}\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n}),

(7.19)

where we denote

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})

\displaystyle:=\left\{b\in\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{s.t.}\hskip 10.00002pt\Big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+jV_{n}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon)\Big|\leqslant\tfrac{4\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})

\displaystyle:=\left\{b\in\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{s.t.}\hskip 10.00002pt\Big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)\Big|\leqslant\tfrac{2\gamma_{n+1}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n})

\displaystyle:=\left\{b\in\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\hskip 10.00002pt\textnormal{s.t.}\hskip 10.00002pt\Big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)\Big|\leqslant\tfrac{\gamma_{n+1}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\right\}.

In view of the inclusion

W^{q,\infty,\gamma}(\mathcal{O},\mathbb{C})\hookrightarrow C^{q-1}(\mathcal{O},\mathbb{C})

and the fact that
q=q_{0}+1
, one obtains that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
the curves

\displaystyle b\mapsto\omega(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+jV_{n}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon),\hskip 10.00002pt(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{(0,0)\}

\displaystyle b\mapsto\omega(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon),\hskip 10.00002pt(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}

\displaystyle b\mapsto\omega(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon),\hskip 10.00002pt(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}

are of regularity
C^{q_{0}}.
Therefore, applying Lemma
3.6
together with Lemma
7.3
yields

\displaystyle\Big|\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}}\langle j\rangle^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\langle l\rangle^{-1-\frac{\tau_{1}+1}{q_{0}}},

\displaystyle\Big|\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n})\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\langle j\rangle^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\langle l\rangle^{-1-\frac{\tau_{1}+1}{q_{0}}},

(7.20)

\displaystyle\Big|\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\langle l\rangle^{-1-\frac{\tau_{2}+1}{q_{0}}}.

We first estimate the measure of
\mathcal{S}_{0}
and
\mathcal{S}_{1}
defined in (
7.2
). From Lemma
7.2
, we have some trivial inclusions allowing us to write for
n\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{n}\lesssim\sum_{\underset{|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,|l|\leqslant N_{n}}{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{(0,0)\}}}\Big|\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\Big|+\sum_{\underset{\underset{\min(|j|,|j_{0}|)\leqslant c_{2}\gamma_{1}^{-\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}{|j-j_{0}|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,|l|\leqslant N_{n}}}{(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}}}\Big|\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})\Big|+\sum_{\underset{|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,|l|\leqslant N_{n}}{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}}\Big|\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n})\Big|.

(7.21)

Inserting (
7.2
) into (
7.21
) implies that for
n\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\Bigg(\sum_{|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle}|j|^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\langle l\rangle^{-1-\frac{\tau_{1}+1}{q_{0}}}+\sum_{\underset{\min(|j|,|j_{0}|)\leqslant c_{2}\gamma^{-\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}{|j-j_{0}|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle}}|j-j_{0}|^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\langle l\rangle^{-1-\frac{\tau_{2}+1}{q_{0}}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\gamma^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}}\sum_{{|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle}}|j|^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\langle l\rangle^{-1-\frac{\tau_{1}+1}{q_{0}}}.

The first two conditions listed in (
7.14
) write

\displaystyle\tau_{1}>d\,q_{0}\hskip 10.00002pt\hbox{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\tau_{2}>\tau_{1}+d\,q_{0}.

(7.22)

Hence, we can make the series appearing in the following expression converge and write

\displaystyle\max_{n\in\{0,1\}}\mathcal{S}_{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\Bigg(\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\langle l\rangle^{-\frac{\tau_{1}}{q_{0}}}+\gamma^{-\upsilon}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}-1-\frac{\tau_{2}}{q_{0}}}\Bigg)+\gamma^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\langle l\rangle^{-\frac{\tau_{1}}{q_{0}}}

(7.23)

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{\min\left(\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}},\frac{1}{q_{0}}-\upsilon\right)}.

Let us now move to the estimate of
\mathcal{S}_{n}
for
n\geqslant 2
defined by (
7.2
). Using Lemma
7.1
and Lemma
7.2
, we infer

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{n}\leqslant\sum_{\underset{|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,N_{n-1}<|l|\leqslant N_{n}}{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{(0,0)\}}}\Big|\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\Big|+\sum_{\underset{\underset{\min(|j|,|j_{0}|)\leqslant c_{2}\gamma_{n+1}^{-\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}{|j-j_{0}|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,N_{n-1}<|l|\leqslant N_{n}}}{(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}}}\Big|\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})\Big|+\sum_{\underset{|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,N_{n-1}<|l|\leqslant N_{n}}{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}}\Big|\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n})\Big|.

Notice that if
|j-j_{0}|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle
and
\min(|j|,|j_{0}|)\leqslant\gamma_{n+1}^{-\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}
, then

\max(|j|,|j_{0}|)=\min(|j|,|j_{0}|)+|j-j_{0}|\leqslant\gamma_{n+1}^{-\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}+C_{0}\langle l\rangle\lesssim\gamma^{-\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}.

Hence, we deduce from (
7.2
) that

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{n}\lesssim\gamma^{\frac{1}{q_{0}}}\Bigg(\sum_{|l|>N_{n-1}}\langle l\rangle^{-\frac{\tau_{1}}{q_{0}}}+\gamma^{-\upsilon}\sum_{|l|>N_{n-1}}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}-1-\frac{\tau_{2}}{q_{0}}}\Bigg)+\gamma^{\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}}\sum_{|l|>N_{n-1}}\langle l\rangle^{-\frac{\tau_{1}}{q_{0}}}.

Now according to (
7.22
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\mathcal{S}_{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{\min\left(\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}},\frac{1}{q_{0}}-\upsilon\right)}.

(7.24)

Inserting (
7.24
) and (
7.23
) into (
7.2
) yields

\Big|(b_{0},b_{1})\setminus\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}\Big|\lesssim\gamma^{\min\left(\frac{\upsilon}{q_{0}},\frac{1}{q_{0}}-\upsilon\right)}.

Remark also that (
7.14
) implies

\min\left(\tfrac{\upsilon}{q_{0}},\tfrac{1}{q_{0}}-\upsilon\right)=\tfrac{\upsilon}{q_{0}}.

Consequently, using the fact that
\gamma=\varepsilon^{a}
due to (
7.2
), we finally get

\Big|(b_{0},b_{1})\setminus\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}\Big|\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{a\upsilon}{q_{0}}}.

This ends the proof of Proposition
7.2
.
∎

We shall now prove Lemmata
7.1
,
7.2
and
7.3
used in the proof of Proposition
7.2
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0,1\}
and
l\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
such that
|l|\leqslant N_{n-1}.
Then the following assertions hold true.

(i)

For
j\in\mathbb{Z}
with
(l,j)\neq(0,0)
, we get
\,\,\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})=\varnothing.

(ii)

For
(j,j_{0})\in(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}
with
(l,j)\neq(0,j_{0}),
we get
\,\,\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})=\varnothing.

(iii)

For
j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}
, we get
\,\,\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n})=\varnothing.

(iv)

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0,1\},

\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}=\bigcup_{\underset{N_{n-1}<|l|\leqslant N_{n}}{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{(0,0)\}}}\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\cup\bigcup_{\underset{N_{n-1}<|l|\leqslant N_{n}}{(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}}}\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})\cup\bigcup_{\underset{N_{n-1}<|l|\leqslant N_{n}}{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}}\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n}).

Proof.

The following estimate, obtained from (
7.5
), turns to be very useful in the sequel. For any
n\geqslant 2
, we have

\displaystyle\|i_{n}-i_{n-1}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|U_{n}-U_{n-1}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|H_{n}\|_{q,s_{h}+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{\ast}\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}.

(7.25)

Since (
7.2
) is only true for
n\geqslant 2
, we had to estimate the measures of
\mathcal{S}_{0}
and
\mathcal{S}_{1}
differently in the proof of Proposition
7.2
.

(i)
Assume that
|l|\leqslant N_{n-1}
and
(l,j)\neq(0,0).
Let us prove that

\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\subset\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n-1}).

(7.26)

Take
b\in\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n}).
In view of (
7.19
), we have in particular that
b\in\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\subset\mathcal{C}_{n-1}^{\varepsilon}.
In addition, the triangle inequality gives

\displaystyle\big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+jV_{n-1}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+jV_{n}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon)\big|+|j|\big|V_{n}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon)-V_{n-1}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{4\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}+C|j|\|V_{i_{n}}^{\infty}-V_{i_{n-1}}^{\infty}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}.

Thus, putting together (
6.27
), (
7.2
), (
7.2
) and the fact that
\sigma_{4}\geqslant 2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+jV_{n-1}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{4\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}+C\varepsilon\langle j\rangle\|i_{n}-i_{n-1}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+2}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{4\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}+C\varepsilon^{2-a}\langle j\rangle N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}.

According the definition of
\gamma_{n}
in Proposition
7.1
-
(\mathcal{P}2)_{n},
we infer

\exists c_{0}>0,\hskip 10.00002pt\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\hskip 10.00002pt\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}-\gamma_{n}^{\upsilon}\leqslant-c_{0}\,\gamma^{\upsilon}2^{-n}.

Notice that (
7.14
), (
7.1
) and (
7.2
) give

\displaystyle 2-a-a\upsilon>1\hskip 10.00002pt\hbox{and}\hskip 10.00002pta_{2}>\tau_{1},

(7.27)

which implies in turn

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}2^{n}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}+\tau_{1}}<\infty.

Consequently, for
\varepsilon
small enough and
|l|\leqslant N_{n-1}
,

\displaystyle\big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+jV_{n-1}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{4\gamma_{n}^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}+C\tfrac{\langle j\rangle\gamma^{\upsilon}}{2^{n}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\Big(-4c_{0}+C\varepsilon 2^{n}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}+\tau_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{4\gamma_{n}^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}.

It follows that
b\in\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n-1})
and this proves (
7.26
). Now, from (
7.19
) we deduce

\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\subset\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n-1})\subset\mathcal{C}_{n-1}^{\varepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}.

In view of (
7.26
) and (
7.19
), we get
\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\subset\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}
and thus we conclude

\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\subset\big(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{n+1}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cap\big(\mathcal{C}_{n-1}^{\varepsilon}\setminus\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\big)=\varnothing.

This proves the first point.

(ii)
Let
(j,j_{0})\in(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}
and
(l,j)\neq(0,j_{0}).
If
j=j_{0}
then by construction
\mathcal{R}_{l,j_{0},j_{0}}(i_{n})=\mathcal{R}_{l,0}^{(0)}(i_{n})
and then the result is an immediate consequence of the first point. Then, we restrict the discussion to the case
j\neq j_{0}.
In a similar way to the point (i), we only have to check that
