\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})\subset\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n-1}).

Take
b\in\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n}).
Then coming back to (
7.19
), we deduce from the triangle inequality that
b\in\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\subset\mathcal{C}_{n-1}^{\varepsilon}
and

\big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(b,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n-1}(b,\varepsilon)\big|\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{2\gamma_{n+1}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}+\varrho^{n}_{j,j_{0}}(b,\varepsilon),

(7.28)

where

\varrho^{n}_{j,j_{0}}(b,\varepsilon):=\big|\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(b,\varepsilon)+\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n-1}(b,\varepsilon)\big|.

According to (
7.2
), one obtains

\displaystyle\varrho^{n}_{j,j_{0}}(b,\varepsilon)\leqslant|j-j_{0}|\big|r^{1,n}(b,\varepsilon)-r^{1,n-1}(b,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle+\big|r_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)-r_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(b,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle+\big|r_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)-r_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n-1}(b,\varepsilon)\big|.

(7.29)

From (
6.54
), (
7.2
), (
7.2
) and the fact that
\sigma_{4}\geqslant\sigma_{3},
we deduce that

\displaystyle\big|r^{1,n}(b,\varepsilon)-r^{1,n-1}(b,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\|i_{n}-i_{n-1}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\sigma_{3}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\gamma^{-1}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2-a}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}.

Similarly, (
6.67
), (
7.2
) and (
7.2
) imply

\displaystyle\big|r_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)-r_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(b,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}\|i_{n}-i_{n-1}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\gamma^{-2}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2(1-a)}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}.

Plugging the preceding two estimates into (
7.2
) yields

\displaystyle\varrho^{n}_{j,j_{0}}(b,\varepsilon)\lesssim\varepsilon^{2(1-a)}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}.

(7.30)

Gathering (
7.30
) and (
7.28
) and using
\gamma_{n+1}=\gamma_{n}-\varepsilon^{a}2^{-n-1},
we obtain

\displaystyle\big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(b,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n-1}(b,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{2\gamma_{n}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}-{\varepsilon^{a}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}2^{-n}\langle l\rangle^{-\tau_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+C\varepsilon^{2(1-a)}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}.

Using the fact that
|l|\leqslant N_{n-1}
, we deduce

\displaystyle-{\varepsilon^{a}}2^{-n}\langle l\rangle^{-\tau_{2}}+C\varepsilon^{2(1-a)}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}\leqslant{\varepsilon^{a}}2^{-n}\langle l\rangle^{-\tau_{2}}\Big(-1+C\varepsilon^{2-3a}2^{n}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}+\tau_{2}}\Big).

Notice that (
7.1
) and (
7.2
) imply in particular

\displaystyle a_{2}>\tau_{2}\hskip 10.00002pt\hbox{and}\hskip 10.00002pta<\tfrac{2}{3}.

(7.31)

Therefore, for
\varepsilon
small enough, we get

\displaystyle\forall\,n\in\mathbb{N},\hskip 10.00002pt-1+C\varepsilon^{2-3a}2^{n}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}+\tau_{2}}\leqslant 0,

which implies in turn

\big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(b,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n-1}(b,\varepsilon)\big|\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{2\gamma_{n}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}\cdot

Finally,
b\in\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n-1}).
This achieves the proof of the second point.

(iii)
Let
j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}.
In particular, one has
(l,j)\neq(0,0).
In a similar line to the first point, we shall prove that if
|l|\leqslant N_{n-1}
and then

\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n})\subset\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n-1}),

where the set
\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n})
is defined below (
7.19
). Take
b\in\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n}).
Then, by construction,
b\in\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\varepsilon}\subset\mathcal{C}_{n-1}^{\varepsilon}.
By using the triangle inequality, (
6.68
), (
7.2
) and the choice
\gamma=\varepsilon^{a}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(b,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)\big|+|\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(b,\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{\gamma_{n+1}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}+C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1}|j|\|i_{n}-i_{n-1}\|_{q,\overline{s}_{h}+\sigma_{4}}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{\gamma_{n+1}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}+C\varepsilon^{2(1-a)}\langle j\rangle N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}}.

Now recalling that
\gamma_{n+1}=\gamma_{n}-\varepsilon^{a}2^{-n-1}
and
|l|\leqslant N_{n-1}
, we get

\big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(b,\varepsilon)\big|\leqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{\gamma_{n}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}+\tfrac{\langle j\rangle\varepsilon^{a}}{2^{n+1}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\Big(-1+\varepsilon^{2-3a}2^{n+1}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}+\tau_{1}}\Big).

As a byproduct of (
7.31
), we infer

\displaystyle a_{2}>\tau_{1}\hskip 10.00002pt\hbox{and}\hskip 10.00002pta<\tfrac{2}{3}.

(7.32)

Therefore, up to take
\varepsilon
small enough, we deduce

\forall\,n\in\mathbb{N},\hskip 10.00002pt-1+\varepsilon^{2-3a}2^{n+1}N_{n-1}^{-a_{2}+\tau_{1}}\leqslant 0,

which implies in turn that

\big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n-1}(b,\varepsilon)\big|\leqslant\tfrac{\gamma_{n}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}.

Finally,
b\in\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n-1})
and the proof of the third point is now complete.

(iv)
Follows immediately from (
7.19
) and the points (i)-(ii)-(iii).
∎

The following lemma provides necessary constraints on the time and space Fourier modes so that the sets in (
7.19
) are not void.

Lemma 7.2
.

There exists
\varepsilon_{0}
such that for any
\varepsilon\in[0,\varepsilon_{0}]
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
the following assertions hold true.

(i)

Let
(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{(0,0)\}.
If
\,\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\neq\varnothing,
then
|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle.

(ii)

Let
(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}.
If
\,\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})\neq\varnothing,
then
|j-j_{0}|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle.

(iii)

Let
(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}.
If
\,\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n})\neq\varnothing,
then
|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle.

(iv)

Let
(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}.
There exists
c_{2}>0
such that if
\displaystyle\min(|j|,|j_{0}|)\geqslant c_{2}\gamma_{n+1}^{-\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}},
then

\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})\subset\mathcal{R}_{l,j-j_{0}}^{(0)}(i_{n}).

Proof.

(i)
Let us assume that
\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(0)}(i_{n})\neq\varnothing.
Then, there exists
b\in(b_{0},b_{1})
such that

|\omega(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+jV_{n}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon)|\leqslant\tfrac{4\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}\langle j\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}.

From triangle and Cauchy-Schwarz inequalities, (
7.6
) and (
7.2
), we deduce

\displaystyle|V_{n}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon)||j|

\displaystyle\leqslant 4|j|\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{-\tau_{1}}+|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l|

\displaystyle\leqslant 4|j|\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}+C\langle l\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant 8\varepsilon^{a\upsilon}|j|+C\langle l\rangle.

Remark that we used the fact that
(b,\varepsilon)\mapsto\omega(b,\varepsilon)
is bounded. Also notice that the identity

V_{n}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon)=\tfrac{1}{2}+r^{1,n}(b,\varepsilon)

together with (
6.20
), (
6.65
) and Proposition
7.1
-
(\mathcal{P}1)_{n}
imply

\displaystyle\forall k\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket,\hskip 10.00002pt\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{b\in(b_{0},b_{1})}|\partial_{b}^{k}r^{1,n}(b,\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle\leqslant\gamma^{-k}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\|r^{1,n}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-k}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{1-ak}.

(7.33)

Hence, taking
\varepsilon
small enough, we infer

\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\inf_{b\in(b_{0},b_{1})}|V_{n}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon)|\geqslant\tfrac{1}{4}.

Therefore, up to choose
\varepsilon
small enough we can ensure
|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle
for some
C_{0}>0.

(ii)
In the case
j=j_{0}
we get by definition
\mathcal{R}_{l,j_{0},j_{0}}(i_{n})=\mathcal{R}^{(0)}_{l,0}(i_{n})
, so this case can be treated by the first point. Then, we shall restrict the discussion to the case
j\neq j_{0}.
Let us assume that
\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n})\neq\varnothing.
Then, there exists
b\in(b_{0},b_{1})
such that

|\omega(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)|\leqslant\tfrac{2\gamma_{n+1}|j-j_{0}|}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}.

By using triangle and Cauchy-Schwarz inequalities, (
7.6
) and (
7.2
), we get

\displaystyle|\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\gamma_{n+1}|j-j_{0}|\langle l\rangle^{-\tau_{2}}+|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\gamma_{n+1}|j-j_{0}|+C\langle l\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant 4\varepsilon^{a}|j-j_{0}|+C\langle l\rangle.

In a similar way to (
7.2
), we may obtain

\displaystyle\forall k\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket,\hskip 10.00002pt\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}\sup_{b\in(b_{0},b_{1})}|j||\partial_{b}^{k}r_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle\leqslant\gamma^{-k}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{j\in\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}}|j|\|r_{j}^{\infty,n}\|_{q}^{\gamma,\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\gamma^{-1-k}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{1-a(1+k)}.

(7.34)

From the triangle inequality, Lemma
3.3
-(iii), (
7.2
) and (
7.2
) we infer for
j\neq j_{0}
,

\displaystyle|\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle\geqslant|\Omega_{j}(b)-\Omega_{j_{0}}(b)|-|r^{1,n}(b,\varepsilon)||j-j_{0}|-|r_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)|-|r_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle\geqslant\big(\tfrac{b_{0}^{2}}{6}-C\varepsilon^{1-a}\big)|j-j_{0}|

\displaystyle\geqslant\tfrac{b_{0}^{2}}{12}|j-j_{0}|.

Notice that the last inequality is obtained for
\varepsilon
sufficiently small. Gathering the previous inequalities implies that, up to choose
\varepsilon
small enough, we can ensure
|j-j_{0}|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,
for some
C_{0}>0.

(iii)
First notice that the case
j=0
is obvious. Now for
j\neq 0
we assume that
\mathcal{R}_{l,j}^{(1)}(i_{n})\neq\varnothing.
Then, there exists
b\in(b_{0},b_{1})
such that

|\omega(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)|\leqslant\tfrac{\gamma_{n+1}|j|}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}.

Thus, triangle and Cauchy-Schwarz inequalities, (
7.6
) and (
7.2
) imply

\displaystyle|\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle\leqslant\gamma_{n+1}|j|\langle l\rangle^{-\tau_{1}}+|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\varepsilon^{a}|j|+C\langle l\rangle.

According to the definition (
7.2
) together with the triangle inequality, Lemma
3.3
-(ii), (
7.2
) and (
7.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle\geqslant\tfrac{b_{0}^{2}}{2}|j|-|j||r^{1,n}(b,\varepsilon)|-|r_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle\geqslant\tfrac{b_{0}^{2}}{2}|j|-C\varepsilon^{1-a}|j|.

Putting together the previous two inequalities and the second condition in (
7.32
) yields

\displaystyle\big(\tfrac{b_{0}^{2}}{2}-C\varepsilon^{1-a}-2\varepsilon^{a}\big)|j|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\langle l\rangle.

Finally, by choosing
\varepsilon
small enough we get
|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,
for some
C_{0}>0.

(iv)
First remark that the case
j=j_{0}
is obvious as a direct consequence of the definition (
7.19
). Let
j\neq j_{0}.
In view of the symmetry property
\mu_{-j}^{\infty,n}=-\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}
of the perturbed eigenvalues, we can always assume that
0<j<j_{0}.
Take
b\in\mathcal{R}_{l,j,j_{0}}(i_{n}).
Then by construction

\big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)\pm\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)\big|\leqslant\tfrac{2\gamma_{n+1}\langle j\pm j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}.

Putting together (
7.2
), (
3.15
) and the triangle inequality, we find

\displaystyle\big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+(j\pm j_{0})V_{n}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)\pm\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)\big|+\tfrac{1}{2}|b^{2j}\pm b^{2j_{0}}|

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\tfrac{1}{2}\big|(j-1)\pm(j_{0}-1)-(j\pm j_{0})\big|+\big|r_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)\pm r_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)\big|.

Hence, we deduce

\displaystyle\big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+(j\pm j_{0})V_{n}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\tfrac{2\gamma_{n+1}\langle j\pm j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}+\tfrac{1}{2}|b^{2j}\pm b^{2j_{0}}|

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\tfrac{1}{2}\big|(j-1)\pm(j_{0}-1)-(j\pm j_{0})\big|+\big|r_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)\pm r_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)\big|.

(7.35)

Notice that

\displaystyle b^{2j}+b^{2j_{0}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\tfrac{\langle j+j_{0}\rangle}{j}.

In addition, Taylor formula implies

\displaystyle b^{2j}-b^{2j_{0}}

\displaystyle\leqslant-2\ln(b)\int_{j}^{j_{0}}b^{2x}dx\leqslant\tfrac{c_{1}\langle j-j_{0}\rangle}{j},

where
c_{1}=\displaystyle\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N},b\in(0,1)}\big(-2\ln(b)jb^{2j}\big)>0.

On the other hand, one has

\big|(j-1)\pm(j_{0}-1)-(j\pm j_{0})\big|=1\pm 1\leqslant\tfrac{\langle j+j_{0}\rangle}{j}.

Applying (
6.65
), we find for
j\neq j_{0}
,

\displaystyle\big|r_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)\pm r_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)\big|\leqslant

\displaystyle C\varepsilon^{1-a}\big(|j|^{-1}+|j_{0}|^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C\varepsilon^{1-a}\tfrac{\langle j\pm j_{0}\rangle}{j}\cdot

Plugging the preceding estimates into (
7.2
) yields

\displaystyle\big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+(j\pm j_{0})V_{n}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon)\big|\leqslant

\displaystyle\tfrac{2\gamma_{n+1}\langle j\pm j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{2}}}+C\tfrac{\langle j\pm j_{0}\rangle}{j}\cdot

Therefore, if we assume
\displaystyle j\geqslant\tfrac{1}{2}C\gamma_{n+1}^{-\upsilon}\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}
and
\tau_{2}>\tau_{1}
, then we deduce

\big|{\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+(j\pm j_{0})V_{n}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon)\big|\leqslant\tfrac{4\gamma_{n+1}^{\upsilon}\langle j\pm j_{0}\rangle}{\langle l\rangle^{\tau_{1}}}\cdot

This achieves the proof of Lemma
7.2
, taking
c_{2}=\frac{C}{2}.

∎

We shall now establish that the perturbed frequencies
\omega(b,\varepsilon)
satisfy the Rüssemann conditions. This is done by a perturbation argument on the transversality conditions of the equilibrium linear frequencies
\omega_{\textnormal{Eq}}(b)
stated in Lemma
3.5
.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
q_{0}
,
C_{0}
and
\rho_{0}
as in Lemma
3.5
. There exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0
small enough such that for any
\varepsilon\in[0,\varepsilon_{0}]
the following assertions hold true.

(i)

For all
l\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\setminus\{0\},
we have

\inf_{b\in[b_{0},b_{1}]}\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{b}^{k}\left({\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l\right)\big|\geqslant\tfrac{\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle}{2}.

(ii)

For all
(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d+1}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
such that
|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,
we have

\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\hskip 10.00002pt\inf_{b\in[b_{0},b_{1}]}\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}|\partial_{b}^{k}\big({\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+jV_{n}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon)\big)|\geqslant\tfrac{\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle}{2}.

(iii)

For all
(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c}
such that
|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,
we have

\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\hskip 10.00002pt\inf_{b\in[b_{0},b_{1}]}\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{b}^{k}\big({\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)\big)\big|\geqslant\tfrac{\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle}{2}.

(iv)

For all
(l,j,j_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times(\mathbb{S}_{0}^{c})^{2}
such that
|j-j_{0}|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle,
we have

\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\hskip 10.00002pt\inf_{b\in[b_{0},b_{1}]}\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{b}^{k}\big({\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)\big)\big|\geqslant\tfrac{\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle}{2}.

Proof.

(i)
From the triangle and Cauchy-Schwarz inequalities together with (
7.13
), (
7.2
) and Lemma
3.5
-(i), we deduce

\displaystyle\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}|\partial_{b}^{k}\left({\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l\right)|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}|\partial_{b}^{k}\left({\omega}_{\textnormal{Eq}}(b)\cdot l\right)|-\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket}|\partial_{b}^{k}\left(\overline{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}(b)\cdot l\right)|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle-C\varepsilon\gamma^{-1-q}N_{0}^{q\overline{a}}\langle l\rangle

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle-C\varepsilon^{1-a(1+q+q\overline{a})}\langle l\rangle

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle}{2}

provided that
\varepsilon
is small enough and

1-a(1+q+q\overline{a})>0.

(7.36)

Notice that the condition (
7.36
) is automatically satisfied by (
7.2
) and (
7.1
).

(ii)
As before, using the triangle and Cauchy-Schwarz inequalities combined with (
7.13
), (
7.2
), Lemma
3.5
-(ii) and the fact that
|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle
, we get

\displaystyle\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}|\partial_{b}^{k}\left({\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+jV_{n}^{\infty}(b,\varepsilon)\right)|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{b}^{k}\big({\omega}_{\textnormal{Eq}}(b)\cdot l+\tfrac{j}{2}\big)\big|-\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket}|\partial_{b}^{k}\left(\overline{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}(b)\cdot l+jr^{1,n}(b,\varepsilon)\right)|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle-C\varepsilon^{1-a(1+q+q\overline{a})}\langle l\rangle-C\varepsilon^{1-aq}|j|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle}{2}

for
\varepsilon
small enough and with the condition (
7.36
).

(iii)
As before, using triangle and Cauchy-Schwarz inequalities combined with (
7.13
), (
7.2
), (
7.2
), Lemma
3.5
-(iii) and the fact that
|j|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle
, we get

\displaystyle\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{b}^{k}\big({\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)\big)\big|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}|\partial_{b}^{k}\left({\omega}_{\textnormal{Eq}}(b)\cdot l+\Omega_{j}(b)\right)|

\displaystyle\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt-\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{b}^{k}\big(\overline{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}(b)\cdot l+jr^{1,n}(b,\varepsilon)+r_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon))\big)\big|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle-C\varepsilon^{1-a(1+q+q\overline{a})}\langle l\rangle-C\varepsilon^{1-a(1+q)}|j|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle}{2}

for
\varepsilon
small enough with the condition (
7.36
).

(iv)
Arguing as in the preceding point, using (
7.2
), (
7.2
), Lemma
3.5
-(iv) and the fact that
0<|j-j_{0}|\leqslant C_{0}\langle l\rangle
(notice that the case
j=j_{0}
is trivial), we have

\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{b}^{k}\big({\omega}(b,\varepsilon)\cdot l

\displaystyle+\mu_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)-\mu_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)\big)\big|\geqslant\displaystyle\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q_{0}\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{b}^{k}\big({\omega}_{\textnormal{Eq}}(b)\cdot l+\Omega_{j}(b)-\Omega_{j_{0}}(b)\big)\big|\displaystyle

\displaystyle-\max_{k\in\llbracket 0,q\rrbracket}\big|\partial_{b}^{k}\big(\overline{\mathrm{r}}_{\varepsilon}(b)\cdot l+(j-j_{0})r^{1,n}(b,\varepsilon)+r_{j}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)-r_{j_{0}}^{\infty,n}(b,\varepsilon)\big)\big|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle-C\varepsilon^{1-a(1+q+q\overline{a})}\langle l\rangle-C\varepsilon^{1-a(1+q)}|j-j_{0}|

\displaystyle\geqslant\displaystyle\tfrac{\rho_{0}\langle l\rangle}{2}

for
\varepsilon
small enough. This ends the proof of Lemma
7.3
.
∎
